## Purpose

This file is the canonical test inventory for the EduAI monorepo. It tracks every test suite across all apps and packages — what exists, what it covers, and what is still missing.

It serves two purposes:

1. **For the team** — a single place to understand the current test coverage picture without having to read every test file individually.
2. **For AI-assisted development** — before writing new tests, reference this file to avoid duplication and to understand coverage gaps. After writing new tests, update this file to keep the inventory current.

## Policy

- Tests are written **before** implementation (test-first approach).
- Every new feature or significant change must have corresponding tests recorded here.
- After any test-related work, update this file to reflect what the tests actually test.

### Coverage

Each app exposes a `test:coverage` script (Vitest V8 coverage), and the monorepo root aggregates them:

```bash
npm run test:coverage   # root — runs coverage for all six suites (backends + frontends) via Turborepo
```

Per app, from the app directory:

| App | Command |
|-----|---------|
| EduAI (core) | `npm run test:coverage` |
| AI Tutor server | `npm run test:coverage` |
| AI Tutor client | `npm run test:coverage` |
| Question Maker backend | `npm run test:coverage` |
| Question Maker frontend | `npm run test:coverage` |
| @eduai/ui | `npm run test:coverage` |

Generated coverage report directories are gitignored.

All three backends measure the unit **and** integration suites together in a single pass via a dedicated `vitest.coverage.config.*` (with `json-summary` + `reportOnFailure` so CI always gets a figure):

- **Question Maker backend** — `src/**` (excluding the `src/index.js` bootstrap and operational `scripts/`). The integration suite needs a PostgreSQL test database via `TEST_DATABASE_URL`; without it the integration tests self-skip and only unit coverage is reported. A `globalSetup` syncs the schema once up front so the shared-worker run is deterministic.
- **AI Tutor server** — `src/**` (excluding `src/index.js`); `globalSetup` runs `prisma migrate deploy` against the `DATABASE_URL` test database.
- **EduAI (core)** — `app/**`; the unit (happy-dom) and integration (node) suites run as two Vitest `projects` so coverage spans both environments.
- **Question Maker frontend** — `src/**` (excluding tests, `*.d.ts`, and the `src/main.tsx` bootstrap); the unit (jsdom) and integration (node) suites run as two Vitest `projects`, mirroring core.

The frontend/UI suites (`@eduai/ui`, AI Tutor client) run coverage through their regular vitest config with the same `json-summary` + `reportOnFailure` settings — no separate coverage config needed since each has a single suite.

### CI coverage report

The **Test Coverage Report** workflow (`.github/workflows/eduai-summer-2026-coverage-report.yml`) runs weekly (Mondays 09:00 UTC) and on manual dispatch. It runs `test:coverage` for all six suites — the three backends against Postgres service containers plus `@eduai/ui`, the AI Tutor client, and the Question Maker frontend — aggregates the `coverage-summary.json` outputs via `eduai-summer-2026/scripts/generate-coverage-report.js`, and commits a Markdown summary to `eduai-summer-2026/reports/coverage/coverage-report.md` on the `docs/coverage` branch. Only the report is committed — coverage build artifacts stay gitignored.

### Per-PR patch coverage

The **Patch coverage** workflow (`.github/workflows/pr-coverage.yml`) runs on every pull request. It runs `test:coverage` for the workspaces the PR touches (`turbo run test:coverage --affected`), then `eduai-summer-2026/scripts/pr-patch-coverage.js` intersects each workspace's `lcov.info` with the PR diff and posts a sticky comment reporting the share of **added or changed** executable lines that a test executed. It is advisory and never blocks merge; the warning threshold is 80%.

Every backend whose suite the workflow measures needs its generated Prisma client present *before* the coverage run, otherwise the imports fail at module load, the suite errors out, and the workspace reports 0% for every touched file rather than reporting nothing. The workflow therefore generates all three (`edu-ai`, AI Tutor server, and Question Maker backend, whose schema outputs to `@eduai/question-maker-prisma-client`) in its "Generate Prisma clients" step. A 0% figure on a file that clearly has tests is the signature of a missing client, not a missing test.

### Prisma client isolation smoke check

Run the repository-level smoke check after generating the AI Tutor server and Question Maker backend Prisma clients:

```bash
npm run test:prisma-client-isolation
```

The check resolves `@eduai/ai-tutor-prisma-client` from the AI Tutor server workspace and `@eduai/question-maker-prisma-client` from the Question Maker backend workspace. It fails if the two workspaces resolve the same generated package or if either package is missing a service-specific model delegate (`aiInteractionTrace` for AI Tutor and `user` for Question Maker). This verifies that generating one extension's Prisma client cannot silently replace the other extension's model set. The pull-request and scheduled coverage workflows run the same check after generating both clients.

## Structure

Tests are organized into three locations across the monorepo:

- There may be some differences because of how some apps are structured, look at the path information to see where tests are stored

```
EduAI/
├── tests/
│   └── e2e/                          # End-to-end tests spanning EduAI and all extensions
├── apps/
│   └── core/
│       └── app/
│           └── tests/
│               ├── unit/             # Unit tests for EduAI
│               └── integration/      # Integration tests for EduAI
└── apps/
    └── extensions/
        └── <extension-name>/
            └── app/
                └── tests/
                    ├── unit/         # Unit tests for the extension
                    └── integration/  # Integration tests for the extension
```

## Populating TESTS.md

Each section below corresponds to a test directory. When you add, change, or remove tests in that directory, update the matching section here.

### What to add per test file

For each test file in a directory, add a row to that section's table. The table should capture:

- **Test file** — the file name (e.g. `auth.test.ts`), linked to its path in the repo
- **What it tests** — a plain-English description of the behaviour being tested

### Table format

Each section should use this format:

```markdown
| Test file | What it tests |
|-----------|---------------|
| `example.test.ts` | Description of what is being tested |
```

### Rules

- One row per test file, not per individual test case.
- The "What it tests" column should describe the **behaviour**, not the implementation — write it so someone unfamiliar with the code understands what would break if this test failed.
- If a test file is added as part of a feature branch, add the row here in the same PR.

### Example

| Test file | What it tests |
|-----------|---------------|
| `auth.test.ts` | JWT token validation and expiry handling |
| `chat-retrieval.test.ts` | RAG pipeline returns course-relevant context for a given query |

## PICT Combinatorial Tests

**Path:** `tests/models/`

PICT (Microsoft's [Pairwise Independent Combinatorial Tool](https://github.com/microsoft/pict)) generates
a minimal table of input combinations covering every pair of parameter values, for surfaces where 3+
independent inputs decide a forked outcome. See [`docs/PICT_CENSUS.md`](docs/PICT_CENSUS.md) for which
surfaces qualify and why.

A model is four pieces:

| File | What it is | Who writes it | Lives in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `<name>.pict` | Parameters, values, constraints (~20 lines) | Author, by hand | `tests/models/` |
| `<name>.cases.json` | Generated rows — **committed**, never hand-edited | `npm run test:pict:gen` | `tests/models/` |
| `<name>.oracle.ts` | Pure function `(row) => expected outcome`, derived from the spec | Author, by hand | `tests/models/` |
| `<name>.test.ts` | World-builder `(row) => seeded state` + `describe.each` over the rows, one adapter per `Path` value | Author, by hand | the consuming app's own integration suite (e.g. `apps/core/app/tests/integration/`) |

The oracle is deliberately app-agnostic (no imports from any app) so a model that spans several
codebases — like the S2 flagship RBAC model — can single-source it; only the world-builder differs
per app, since it needs that app's auth mocks, factories, and route handlers. The world-builder test
file imports the oracle and the generated cases by relative path from `tests/models/`.

The oracle must be derived from the spec, never read off the handler under test — copying the
handler's branch logic makes the test tautological (it asserts the code does what the code does).
This escape hatch is narrow and easy to abuse: it is legitimate for an adapter to defer to a
documented platform-wide convention only when that convention is written down *and citable by
line* in a spec doc (e.g. a specific line in `docs/implementations/rbac-matrix.md` §3), and the
oracle's comment must link that line, not the implementation symbol that happens to agree with it.
"The handler already does X" is never itself a citation — if the only source for a value is the
handler under test, the oracle is tautological regardless of how the deferral is worded. Where a
real disagreement between the spec and a handler surfaces this way, prefer fixing the handler (or
updating the spec, if the handler is actually correct) over encoding the handler's behavior into
the oracle.
Where the spec is genuinely silent on a presentational detail (e.g. which of several "denied" HTTP
status codes a REST adapter returns), it's legitimate for the adapter to defer to a documented
platform-wide convention (e.g. the 404-vs-403 split in `docs/implementations/rbac-matrix.md` §3) —
that's a shared contract, not the handler under test.

### Generation runs in Docker, not a host install

`npm run test:pict:gen` always runs `pict` inside the pinned `docker/pict` image — rebuilding it
every run (fast no-op via Docker's own layer cache once the image is current) rather than trusting
whatever a local image with that tag happens to be — instead of a natively-installed `pict` binary.
This isn't incidental: PICT's greedy
solver breaks ties between equally-valid candidate rows via hash-container iteration order, which
differs across C++ standard library implementations. A macOS/Homebrew build (clang + libc++) and a
Linux build (g++ + libstdc++) produce a **different row count** for the identical model — 19 rows
vs 18 for this repo's pilot model, not just a reordering. Since the generated JSON is committed and
diffed, every environment producing it must agree byte-for-byte, so generation only ever happens
through the one pinned image. Docker is already a required dependency for this repo (see the root
README's "Getting started"), so this adds no new local prerequisite.

If you want a `pict` binary on your host to experiment interactively with a model's shape before
running the real generator, `brew install pict` (macOS) works fine for that — just never treat its
output as authoritative, and always run `npm run test:pict:gen` before committing.

### Adding a model

1. Create `tests/models/<name>.pict` — parameter list, values, and any `IF ... THEN ...`
   constraints. PICT's own model syntax; see existing models for examples.
2. Run `npm run test:pict:gen`. This writes `tests/models/<name>.cases.json`: an array of
   `{ParamName: "value"}` objects, one per row, in column order.
3. Commit both files. A model change then shows up in review as a plain row diff, not an invisible
   behavior change, and CI needs nothing beyond Docker (already preinstalled on GitHub-hosted
   runners) to regenerate and verify it.

Optional sidecar `tests/models/<name>.config.json` controls generation:

```json
{ "order": 3, "seed": "<name>.seed.tsv" }
```

- `order` — PICT's `/o:N` (2 = pairwise, the default when omitted; 3 = triples; higher toward
  exhaustive).
- `seed` — PICT's `/e:<file>`, a path relative to `tests/models/` to a TSV of rows (header + data,
  same column names as the model) that must appear in the output. Use this to pin a specific
  regression combination into a model permanently.

### Regenerating

```bash
npm run test:pict:gen
```

Regenerates every `tests/models/*.cases.json` from its `.pict` (and optional `.config.json`)
source. Generation is deterministic — the script never passes a random seed, and always runs the
same pinned image — so re-running on an unchanged model produces byte-identical output. If the
`docker` CLI isn't on `PATH`, the script fails immediately with an install pointer rather than
silently skipping.

### CI drift check

The `pict-drift` job in `pr-tests.yml` runs the generator (via the same Docker image) and fails the
PR if that dirties `tests/models/` — covering both a stale `.cases.json` (source changed, output
didn't) and a new `.pict` committed without its generated sibling. Without this check a stale case
table silently tests the old row set, which is worse than no coverage because it still looks green.

### Current models

| Model | Dims | What it covers |
|---|---|---|
| [`material-visibility.pict`](tests/models/material-visibility.pict) | 6 | Material read-gate inputs (role, enrollment, publish state, delete state, read path) across the REST route (`routes/api/courses.materials.$.ts`) and both RAG retrieval branches (`lib/ai/embedding.ts` — hybrid BM25 and pure vector) — census § S1 pilot, the first model to land end to end: [`material-visibility.oracle.ts`](tests/models/material-visibility.oracle.ts) (pure verdict function) and the world-builder + REST/RAG adapters in [`apps/core/app/tests/integration/material-visibility.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/material-visibility.integration.test.ts). One caveat: this model's 18-row pairwise reduction never isolates `AvailableAt=future` as the sole reason for denial (every row combining it with a `STUDENT` role also sets `Deleted=yes` or `VisibleToStudents=false`) — a real limit of pairwise-vs-exhaustive coverage, not a gap in the oracle or adapters. |
| [`course-access-across-apps.pict`](tests/models/course-access-across-apps.pict) | 5 | Cross-app per-course RBAC drift contract (census § S2 / #1181): `shared_course_rbac ∩ app_role_floor` via [`course-access-across-apps.oracle.ts`](tests/models/course-access-across-apps.oracle.ts). `App` is an adapter parameter (not a PICT dimension) — every generated row is replayed ×3 through Core [`course-access-across-apps.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/course-access-across-apps.integration.test.ts) (`resolveCourseAccessWithCourse` + publish gate), QM [`course-access-across-apps.test.js`](apps/extensions/question-maker/app/backend/tests/unit/course-access-across-apps.test.js) (`QM_AUTHORIZED` + `resolveAccessForCourse`), and AI Tutor [`course-access-across-apps.test.js`](apps/extensions/ai-tutor/server/tests/integration/course-access-across-apps.test.js) (`GET /courses/:courseId`). Known AT drift (#1405): detail membership skips the student publish gate — adapter asserts the buggy HTTP 200 allow for `unpublished-student` rows (not a broad `it.fails`). |
| [`material-visibility.pict`](tests/models/material-visibility.pict) | 6 | Material read-gate inputs (role, enrollment, visible-to-students flag, availability window, delete state, Canvas-unpublished state) — census § S1 pilot: [`material-visibility.oracle.ts`](tests/models/material-visibility.oracle.ts) (pure verdict function) exercised against all three read paths — REST (`routes/api/courses.materials.$.ts`) and both RAG retrieval branches (`lib/ai/embedding.ts` — hybrid BM25 and pure vector) — for every row, in [`apps/core/app/tests/integration/material-visibility.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/material-visibility.integration.test.ts). `Path` is deliberately not a model dimension: the test cross-products all three paths per row itself, so any two paths disagreeing on the same input fails loudly instead of silently, which a per-row `Path` factor (routing each row to exactly one path) could not have caught. A [`.config.json`](tests/models/material-visibility.config.json) seed pins the `STUDENT`-visible and isolated-denial-reason rows pairwise alone wouldn't guarantee. This pilot surfaced two real disagreements between paths, both fixed as part of landing the contract (per #1180 — not left for a follow-up issue): the REST single-material read returned 404 instead of 403 for a hidden-but-existing material (indistinguishable from nonexistent, because the student-visibility gate was folded into the same `WHERE` clause as the existence check); and RAG's Canvas-unpublished filter applied to every caller including staff, while REST's staff bypass skipped it entirely — a Canvas-unpublished material was readable by an instructor over REST but invisible to that same instructor in RAG. |

| [`password-set-reuse-gate.pict`](tests/models/password-set-reuse-gate.pict) | 6 | The #339 before-hook in `lib/auth/server.ts` gating strength + no-reuse-of-last-10 on every password-setting path (census § S6 / #1185): `path × strength × reset-token × session × current-password × reuse`, encoding the documented precedence that a wrong `/change-password` current-password beats the reuse check — [`password-set-reuse-gate.oracle.ts`](tests/models/password-set-reuse-gate.oracle.ts) and the real-`auth.handler()` adapter in [`apps/core/app/tests/integration/password-set-reuse-gate.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/password-set-reuse-gate.integration.test.ts). Finding: `/set-password` is a better-auth `SERVER_ONLY` endpoint with no `ctx.path`, so the hook's `PASSWORD_SETTING_PATHS` lookup never matches it and the gate silently never runs there — filed as [#1385](https://github.com/EduAI-Lab/EduAI/issues/1385), `it.fails` rows. |
| [`auth-precedence.pict`](tests/models/auth-precedence.pict) | 3 | Drift-override model (census § S6 / #1185) combining `enforceAdminIfApiKey` (`lib/auth/guards.server.ts`) with its composition at `GET /api/me`: `KeyState × CookieState × Site`, converting the hand-written matrix in `guards.server.test.ts:125-200` into a generated table with a site dimension so the two call sites can never silently drift — [`auth-precedence.oracle.ts`](tests/models/auth-precedence.oracle.ts) and the mocked-auth adapter in [`apps/core/app/tests/unit/auth-precedence.pict.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/auth-precedence.pict.test.ts). |
| [`role-forked-listing.pict`](tests/models/role-forked-listing.pict) | 5 | Drift-override model (census § S6 / #1185): does course C appear in the caller's list? Core `buildCourseListFilter` (`lib/auth/course-access.server.ts`) keys the non-admin branches on ENROLLMENT role only; ai-tutor's `GET /courses` (`routes/courses.js`) forks on platform role instead — `PlatformRole × Enrollment × Published × UnitMatch × Site`, with the `STUDENT`+enrollment-role-`instructor` row pinned via [`role-forked-listing.seed.tsv`](tests/models/role-forked-listing.seed.tsv) so it's never dropped by the pairwise reduction — [`role-forked-listing.oracle.ts`](tests/models/role-forked-listing.oracle.ts) (`courseVisibleCore`/`courseVisibleAiTutor`) with adapters in [`apps/core/app/tests/integration/role-forked-listing.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/role-forked-listing.integration.test.ts) and [`apps/extensions/ai-tutor/server/tests/integration/role-forked-listing.test.js`](apps/extensions/ai-tutor/server/tests/integration/role-forked-listing.test.js). Finding: a platform-`STUDENT` holding instructor-of-record status is visible in Core's listing but not ai-tutor's — both sides are internally correct per their own rule, so no row fails; filed as [#1386](https://github.com/EduAI-Lab/EduAI/issues/1386) for a product decision. |
| [`resolve-chat-read-access.pict`](tests/models/resolve-chat-read-access.pict) | 6 | The §5c single-chat oversight gate `resolveChatReadAccess` (`lib/chat-history/server.ts`, census § S7 / #1186), shared by `GET /api/chats/:id` and `/api/chats/:id/messages`: `owner × admin × courseId × access-level × policy-flag × owner-active-student` — owner/ADMIN always read; otherwise a course-scoped viewer needs a policy-open instructor/unit access level AND the chat owner to be an active enrolled STUDENT — [`resolve-chat-read-access.oracle.ts`](tests/models/resolve-chat-read-access.oracle.ts) and the world-builder in [`apps/core/app/tests/integration/resolve-chat-read-access.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/resolve-chat-read-access.integration.test.ts). |
| [`create-question.pict`](tests/models/create-question.pict) | 5 | `createQuestion`'s (`lib/questions/server.ts`, census § S7 / #1186) 6-way error taxonomy (5 errors + success): `Validity × Course × PrimaryTopic × SecondaryTopicIds × Type`, encoding the precedence schema → course → primary-topic → exact-duplicate → secondary-topic-validity → success, with missing and soft-deleted secondary topic ids folded into one `INVALID_TOPIC_IDS` outcome — [`create-question.oracle.ts`](tests/models/create-question.oracle.ts) and the real-Postgres adapter in [`apps/core/app/tests/integration/create-question.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/create-question.integration.test.ts). |
| [`admin-write-confirmation.pict`](tests/models/admin-write-confirmation.pict) | 5 | The admin-chat write confirmation gate (census § S7 / #1186): models the real caller-facing entry point `runConfirmedAdminWriteTool` (`lib/agent-tools/admin-mutations.server.ts`), not just the underlying preview map — `Confirmed × PreviewState × Identity × ConfirmTurn × RunOutcome`, covering one-time preview consumption, cross-actor/tool/payload key isolation, and the same-turn anti-replay guard — [`admin-write-confirmation.oracle.ts`](tests/models/admin-write-confirmation.oracle.ts) and the in-memory adapter in [`apps/core/app/tests/unit/admin-write-confirmation.pict.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/admin-write-confirmation.pict.test.ts). |

## EduAI Full Platform End-to-End Tests

**Path:** `tests/e2e/tests/`

> Most E2E coverage is API-only. Browser UI tests are added selectively for high-risk regressions (see `core/chat-code-block.spec.ts`).

| Test file | What it tests |
|-----------|---------------|
| [`ai-tutor/access.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/ai-tutor/access.spec.ts) | AI Tutor server health (`GET /api/health`); unauthenticated calls to `/api/me` and `/api/courses` return 401; authenticated user (Core session cookie) can call `/api/me` and `/api/courses`; STUDENT blocked from admin routes; `POST /api/logout` proxies sign-out to Core and invalidates the session; idempotent logout with no session. |
| [`ai-tutor/dashboard-submissions.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/ai-tutor/dashboard-submissions.spec.ts) | Smoke for the redesigned `/dashboard` landing and instructor Submissions tab (#938): `GET /api/me/dashboard-stats` 401 unauthenticated, STUDENT-shaped rollup (enrolledCourses + correctAnswerPercentage), INSTRUCTOR-shaped rollup (yourCourses + submissionsToReview); `GET /api/courses/:id/submissions` 401 unauthenticated, INSTRUCTOR reads an empty enriched array for their imported course, and an unenrolled STUDENT is 403. |
| [`ai-tutor/content-lifecycle.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/ai-tutor/content-lifecycle.spec.ts) | INSTRUCTOR creates courses, modules, and lessons (all start unpublished); publish hierarchy pre-conditions — module publish blocked when parent course is unpublished (400), lesson publish blocked when parent module is unpublished (400); enrolled STUDENT cannot see an unpublished course; STUDENT sees course/module/lesson after INSTRUCTOR publishes each level in order; INSTRUCTOR sees unpublished modules while STUDENT does not; cascade unpublish — unpublishing a course sets all child modules and lessons to unpublished; unpublishing a module cascades to its lessons; AI Tutor admin endpoints: `GET /api/admin/users` → 200, `GET /api/admin/courses` → 200, `PATCH /api/admin/users/:id/role` → 410 (roles managed by EduAI). |
| [`ai-tutor/rbac.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/ai-tutor/rbac.spec.ts) | AI Tutor course mutation gates (STUDENT → 403 for POST/PATCH courses, import-external, `GET /api/eduai/courses`, module and lesson creation, `PATCH /courses/:id/publish`, `PATCH /courses/:id/unpublish`, and `GET /courses/:id/feedback`); admin route gates (STUDENT → 403 for admin users, courses, settings, bug-reports, role-change); routes open to all authenticated users (bug reports 201, course list 200); unauthenticated 401 on protected routes. |
| [`core/access-control.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/core/access-control.spec.ts) | Unauthenticated requests to all protected Core routes return 401; STUDENT role: can read own profile, gets empty course list (no enrollments), can read and PATCH assistive preferences; STUDENT is blocked (403) from admin-only AI-provider/model lists, course creation, and invitation management; sign-out then re-sign-in restores access; service key validate round-trip. |
| [`core/admin.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/core/admin.spec.ts) | ADMIN retrieves the full user list with correct field shapes (id, email, role, isActive) and sees newly registered users; ADMIN can promote another user to INSTRUCTOR (target sees new role after re-sign-in); ADMIN cannot change their own role (403); ADMIN can deactivate another user; full invitation happy path (create → validate token → accept → sign in with new role); ADMIN lists pending invitations without exposing tokenHash; token reuse after acceptance is rejected; ADMIN can revoke a pending invitation. |
| [`core/chat-code-block.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/core/chat-code-block.spec.ts) | Browser regression for #667: enrolled student on `/chat` receives a mocked streamed assistant reply with a fenced `js` code block; after streaming completes, Streamdown **Copy Code** writes the snippet to the clipboard and **Download file** saves the code. |
| [`core/courses.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/core/courses.spec.ts) | ADMIN creates / reads / updates / publishes / unpublishes / soft-deletes a Core course; STUDENT blocked from course creation (403); enrolled STUDENT sees published course in list and via direct GET, is denied (403) when course is unpublished, and sees nothing once unpublished again; unenrolled STUDENT cannot see any course regardless of publish state; ADMIN enrolls a STUDENT and the enrollment appears in the list; STUDENT cannot self-enroll (403). |
| [`core/rbac.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/core/rbac.spec.ts) | Core admin-only route gates (STUDENT → 403 for user list, admin bug reports, AI providers/models, Ollama/vLLM models); invitation management gates (STUDENT → 403 for GET/POST invitations); course creation blocked for STUDENT; bug-report ownership scope (`GET /api/bug-reports?mine=true` returns 200, without `mine=true` returns 400); unauthenticated 401/403 on protected routes; role escalation prevention (`PATCH /api/me` silently ignores `role` changes to ADMIN/INSTRUCTOR). |
| [`core/registration.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/core/registration.spec.ts) | Core user registration (sign-up rejects duplicate email, short password, invalid email), sign-in success/failure, `GET /api/me` auth gate and profile shape, `PATCH /api/me` name update and role-change rejection, `POST /api/sessions/validate` 200/401/405, and sign-out session invalidation. |
| [`cross-service/cascade-delete-propagation.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/cross-service/cascade-delete-propagation.spec.ts) | §802: a Core course assigned to an INSTRUCTOR is mirrored into both QM (auto-import-on-list) and AI Tutor (`import-external`); both mirrors are confirmed reachable (200); the course is then deleted in Core (204); Core's cascade push is fire-and-forget and does not block that response, so both the QM course (`GET /api/course/:id`) and the AI Tutor offering (`GET /api/courses/:id`) are confirmed 404 via `expect.poll`. |
| [`cross-service/publish-propagation.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/cross-service/publish-propagation.spec.ts) | Shared user identity — student enrolled in AT by their Core user ID can see a published AT course via their Core session; enrollment independence — enrolling a student in a Core course does NOT auto-enroll them in a native AT course (student sees Core course but not the AT one); role propagation — INSTRUCTOR promoted in Core can immediately create AT content with no separate AT login; ADMIN promoted in Core can access AT admin-only routes; STUDENT blocked from AT INSTRUCTOR routes. |
| [`cross-service/user-journey.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/cross-service/user-journey.spec.ts) | One Core session cookie grants access to Core, AI Tutor, and QM simultaneously with matching email/role; all three reject with 401 when unauthenticated; Core sign-out cascades to all three; AI Tutor logout cascades to Core; QM logout cascades to Core; re-authentication restores access to all three; two-user data isolation on QM courses; two-user profile isolation on Core. |
| [`question-maker/access.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/question-maker/access.spec.ts) | QM health endpoints (`GET /healthz`, `GET /`, unknown route 404); unauthenticated calls to `/api/auth/me`, `/api/course`, `/api/questions` return 401; authenticated user: `/api/auth/me` returns user with `isBugReportAdmin=false`, `/api/course` and `/api/questions` return arrays, can create and retrieve a course; `POST /api/auth/logout` proxies to Core; STUDENT blocked from question create (403) and assessment create (403). |
| [`question-maker/rbac.spec.ts`](tests/e2e/tests/question-maker/rbac.spec.ts) | QM question route gates (STUDENT → 403 for GET/POST questions and `GET /api/questions/:id`); assessment route gates (STUDENT → 403 for list, create, get, and get-questions operations); course routes open to all authenticated users (list, create, own-course GET returns 200); unauthenticated 401 on protected routes; cross-user data isolation (one user cannot read another user's QM course by ID — 403 or 404). |

## EduAI Unit Tests

**Path:** `apps/core/app/tests/unit/`

| Test file | What it tests |
|-----------|---------------|
| [`reembed-course-materials.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/reembed-course-materials.test.ts) | Bounded-concurrency `reEmbedCourseMaterials` (#945, prisma + `ai`'s `embedMany` mocked so the real function/loop runs): never exceeds `REINDEX_CONCURRENCY` (default 4) materials in flight at once, proven concurrent (not serial) via an in-flight counter around a delayed `embedMany`; one material's embedding failure is isolated — it's marked `FAILED` while siblings still complete and are marked `READY`, and the run's `processed`/`failed`/`total` aggregate matches the per-material outcomes; `onProgress` snapshots stay monotonic in `processed` and the final snapshot matches the returned result even though completion order is no longer sequential; materials with blank/null `rawText` are excluded from `eligible`/`total`. |
| [`resolve-pool.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/resolve-pool.test.ts) | (#1351/#1418) `resolveQueueName`'s pool selection agrees with the fleet registry's config-file-first, env-var-fallback resolution instead of checking `VLLM_FLEET_HEAVY_URL` directly: env-var-only fallback (interactive always chat, background shares chat until the heavy env var is set, then resolves to heavy); config-file-only (a background server declared only in `fleet.config.json`, with no heavy env var set, correctly resolves `background` to the heavy queue; a stale heavy env var left over from an unmigrated `.env` does not leak through once a config file with no background-capable server is present); and `priorityFor`'s interactive-before-background ordering. |
| [`queue.stats.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/queue.stats.test.ts) | Queue depth/position reads + backpressure policy (#915, prisma mocked): `getQueueDepth` counts PENDING rows by the persisted `queueName` (scoped to the requested queue, not the current type mapping), `getQueuePosition` returns a 1-based position counting stronger-priority and earlier same-priority jobs in the same queue as ahead (id tiebreak on identical `createdAt`; null once not PENDING) and stays on the row's persisted queue even after `VLLM_FLEET_HEAVY_URL` is flipped, falling back to the live mapping only when `queueName` is null, both reads exclude stale failed-add orphans (PENDING, no `bullJobId`, older than the grace window), `maxQueueDepth` treats unset/blank/`0`/junk/partial-numerics (`1e3`, `10k`) as disabled, and `QueueFullError` carries queue/depth/cap/`Retry-After`; `getQueueSnapshot` reads the pair inside one `REPEATABLE READ` transaction (asserted explicitly — the READ COMMITTED default would let jobs draining between the two counts push position past depth), pins a single grace-window cutoff across both reads (proven with fake timers, since a real clock leaves both in the same millisecond), and still returns a null position for a non-PENDING row. |
| [`csp-nonce-document.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/csp-nonce-document.test.ts) | Every inline `<script>` in a document response carries the CSP nonce the same response's `script-src` allows (#1219). Renders the real `root.tsx` through `entry.server`'s `handleRequest` and pins each nonce that is drilled in by hand and would otherwise regress silently: React Router's SSR data-stream scripts (`streamController.enqueue`/`.close()`, from `<ServerRouter nonce>`), next-themes' no-flash script (from `<ThemeProvider nonce>`), and React's own Suspense-reveal scripts (`$RC`/`$RV`/`$RB`, from the `nonce` render option). Fails if any inline script ships unnonced, which under strict CSP means the browser drops it. |
| [`queue.job-schema.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/queue.job-schema.test.ts) | `JobPayloadSchema` validation for the AI-job queue (#914): accepts a valid `question-generation` payload, rejects an unknown `kind`, rejects when `input.kind` disagrees with the top-level `kind`, enforces the 1..100 integer `count` bound, rejects an invalid `type` and an empty `source`, and allows the optional `requestedModel` / `idempotencyKey`. |
| [`queue.enqueue.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/queue.enqueue.test.ts) | `enqueue()` producer (#914/#915/#1112/#1269 review, prisma/queue/logging mocked): creates a `PENDING` `AiJob` row with `bullJobId` set to `idempotencyKey` **at create time** for a keyed job (never persisted after `queue.add`, which always echoes that same id back), adds to the resolved pool queue with that job id, maps `background`→priority 10 / `interactive`→priority 1, returns the DB `AiJob.id`, rejects an invalid payload before any DB write, and on a queue-add failure **marks the row FAILED (not deleted)** and throws `QueueUnavailableError`, so a same-key retry finds the FAILED row (already carrying its `bullJobId` from create) instead of minting a second one. A concurrent enqueue on the same key now races at `create()` — the loser returns the winner's row without ever calling `queue.add()` — instead of racing post-add; an unkeyed job still persists BullMQ's auto-generated id after `add()` purely for tracking, which cannot conflict the way a keyed id could. Also wraps DB-unreachable create failures as `QueueUnavailableError`, and (#915) returns a live `queuePosition`/`queueDepth` snapshot from a post-write read (degrading to nulls when that read fails, since the job is already durably enqueued) while enforcing `QUEUE_MAX_DEPTH` backpressure before any write. |
| [`queue.errors.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/queue.errors.test.ts) | `#1112` error classification: Redis/`ECONNREFUSED` and Prisma `P1001`-class codes are infrastructure → HTTP 503; Zod validation → 400; other errors → 500; `toQueueUnavailable` preserves `cause`. |
| [`re-embed-job.start.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/re-embed-job.start.test.ts) | `startReEmbedJob` consistency (#1112/#1269 review): creates PENDING then awaits claim→RUNNING, reuses active jobs and **course-scoped** idempotency keys without a second create, resolves `P2002` idempotency races to the winning row, compensates (deletes) when the claim boundary fails and logs rather than swallows a failure of that delete itself, replays a terminal row on the same key within the 24h retry window (`created: false`) and recycles it into a fresh run once that window has passed, reclaims a stale (30+ min silent) `RUNNING` row **or a stale (5+ min) `PENDING` row** instead of returning either as active, recycles a stale row found *by key* directly rather than falling through to a duplicate-key `create()`, reports `keyHonored: false` (not silent success) when an active job already carries a different caller's key or a concurrent attach loses that race, surfaces `QueueUnavailableError` — without reclaiming or attaching a key — when the stale-reclaim write or the best-effort key-attach onto an active job hits an infra failure, and wraps DB-unreachable errors as `QueueUnavailableError`. |
| [`courses.re-embed.route.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/courses.re-embed.route.test.ts) | Re-embed route (#1112/#1269 review): 202 on create, 200 on idempotent retry (including `keyHonored` when an `Idempotency-Key` was supplied), **503** on Redis/DB outage (`QueueUnavailableError`), 401/404 for auth/access. |
| [`re-embed-job.consistency.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/re-embed-job.consistency.integration.test.ts) | Re-embed consistency integration (#1112/#1269 review): idempotent retry → one row, course-scoped keys, concurrent duplicates → one row, claim-failure compensation → zero orphan PENDING, stale-`RUNNING` reclaim starts fresh, terminal-row replay within the retry window vs. recycle once it has passed, and idempotency-key attach onto an active job found without a key match. |
| [`queue.enqueue.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/queue.enqueue.test.ts) | `enqueue()` producer (#914, prisma/queue/logging mocked): creates a `PENDING` `AiJob` row, adds to the resolved pool queue (`ai-jobs-chat`) with the BullMQ job name = kind, maps `background`→priority 10 / `interactive`→priority 1, applies the env-configurable BullMQ retry policy, passes `idempotencyKey` through as the BullMQ job id, persists the returned `bullJobId`, returns the DB `AiJob.id`, rejects an invalid payload before any DB write, and on a queue-add failure leaves the row without a `bullJobId`. |
| [`queue.worker.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/queue.worker.test.ts) | Async AI-job dequeue/dispatch worker (#168/#916): feeds the producer-emitted queue envelope directly into the processor, verifies durable-id lookup with composite fallback after an idempotency conflict, rejects duplicate `RUNNING` delivery claims, covers atomic `PENDING → RUNNING → COMPLETED`, cancellation preservation, transient `RUNNING → PENDING` retry, final `RUNNING → FAILED`, job-name/payload-kind agreement, fleet-aware question-generation execution with an abort budget, and per-pool concurrency. (#1351/#1418) Also covers `executeAiJobPayload` persisting `AIInteraction` telemetry after a successful completion — previously async Question Maker jobs never wrote a row at all, so they were invisible to the admin Servers tab's per-server/per-model totals: asserts the persisted `serverId` matches the fleet server that served the job (or `null` when not fleet-routed), and that a telemetry write failure never fails the job itself. |
| [`queue.worker.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/queue.worker.integration.test.ts) | Real Redis/Postgres AI-job lifecycle verification required by #916: enqueues through a BullMQ `Queue`, consumes through a real `Worker`, observes the durable Prisma row as `PENDING` before worker start and `RUNNING` inside execution, then verifies the persisted `COMPLETED` result and attempt metadata. |
| [`queue.enqueue.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/queue.enqueue.test.ts) | `enqueue()` producer (#914, prisma/queue/logging mocked): creates a `PENDING` `AiJob` row, adds to the resolved pool queue (`ai-jobs:chat`) with the BullMQ job name = kind, maps `background`→priority 10 / `interactive`→priority 1, passes `idempotencyKey` through as the BullMQ job id, persists the returned `bullJobId`, returns the DB `AiJob.id`, rejects an invalid payload before any DB write, and on a queue-add failure leaves the row without a `bullJobId`; #915: returns a live `queuePosition`/`queueDepth` snapshot (degrading to nulls when the post-enqueue stats read fails, since the job is already durably enqueued), rejects with `QueueFullError` at `QUEUE_MAX_DEPTH` before any write, never caps when the var is unset, never rejects an idempotent replay, and on a failed queue add deletes a keyed row (so the replay isn't blocked) while keeping an unkeyed one for the reaper, and returns the winner's own snapshot when a same-queue `bullJobId` race drops its row; the two stats reads are routed by query shape rather than call order, so the happy path asserts a depth that counts the job itself and a dedicated case proves depth comes from a post-write read instead of the pre-write backpressure count (which could otherwise report `queuePosition > queueDepth`). |
| [`chat-enqueue.route.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/chat-enqueue.route.test.ts) | `POST /api/chat` producer branch (#914/#915, `enqueueQuestionGeneration` mocked but `isEnqueueRequested`/`QueueFullError` real): returns 202 with `jobId` + the live `queuePosition`/`queueDepth` snapshot and never calls `streamText`, passes a degraded null snapshot through on a 202, maps `QueueFullError` to 429 with a `Retry-After` header and `retryAfterSeconds` body field, maps a queue/Redis outage to 502 with no `Retry-After`, and leaves the branch entirely when `QUEUE_ENQUEUE_ENABLED` is unset even though the request opts in. |
| [`ai-job-status.route.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/ai-job-status.route.test.ts) | `GET /api/ai-jobs/:jobId` (#917, auth/prisma/queue-stats mocked): returns the caller-owned job with a live re-derived `queuePosition` (never a stale enqueue-time snapshot), 404s rather than 403s on another user's job id so the response can't confirm the id exists, 401s an unauthenticated request without touching Prisma, and — for the synthetic `service` user Question Maker's server-to-server jobs are created under — accepts a valid `EDUAI_API_KEY`/service-key caller to poll its own service-owned jobs while passing through the guard's own error response for an invalid key. |
| [`ChatScreen.test.tsx`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/ChatScreen.test.tsx) | Core chat screen header and stored routed-model metadata, plus the persisted-chat course-switch regression: selecting another course navigates through `/chat?courseCode=...` and renders a blank chat with the new course selected. |
| [`chat-course-change.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/chat-course-change.test.ts) | Course selection behavior in Core chat: changing course in a persisted chat starts a new course-scoped chat, changing course before persistence updates in place, and selecting the current course is a no-op. |

| `logging.server.test.ts` | The logging facade redaction: `logAuditAction` replaces credential- and PII-shaped keys (`password`, `phone`, `apiKey`, `secret`, `clientSecret`, `privateKey`, `jwt`, `databaseUrl`/`dbUrl`/`databaseUri`, `passcode`/`passphrase`, `otp`/`totp`/`dsn`/`mfa`/`mfaCode`/`mfaRecoveryCode`/`pin`, `sessionId`, etc.) with `[REDACTED]` while keeping accountability IDs and full emails; short tokens (`auth`/`pin`/`otp`/`dsn`/`totp`/`mfa`) use segment-exact matching so ordinary fields (`authorId`, `mapping`, `shippingAddress`, `hotPath`, `footprint`, `fieldsName`, `needsNormalization`) stay visible; MFA status flags (`mfaEnabled`/`mfaRequired`/`mfaEnrolled`/`mfaStatus`) stay visible via a safe-key allowlist; table-driven positive/negative cases; handles circular references (`[CIRCULAR]`) and Map/Set values without overflowing; `logSecurityEvent` forces the SECURITY category and still logs full emails; `logSystemError` routes through the centralized helper with redacted details. Also covers #976: all three facade calls now use value-level `sanitizeSensitiveData`, so a secret under an innocuous key is scrubbed too, and every `console.error` fallback passes the caught error through `redactErrorForConsole`. |
| `db.auditlog.server.test.ts` | The audit-log data layer: `createAuditLog` stable defaults (`outcome: SUCCESS`, `actorType: USER`), `createSecurityLog` forcing `category: SECURITY`, `listAuditLogs` excluding SECURITY rows by default vs `listSecurityLogs` scoping to them, `getAuditLogById` including the actor relation, and `deleteAuditLogsOlderThan` / `runAuditLogRetention` using timestamp cutoffs so recent rows survive cleanup. |
| `db.systemlog.server.test.ts` | The system-log data layer: `createSystemLog` write path, fire-and-forget fallback to `console.error` when the DB write fails, `createSystemError` deriving `level: ERROR` / `errorName` / `stack` from an `Error`, paginated and level-filtered `listSystemLogs`, and `deleteSystemLogsOlderThan` / `runSystemLogRetention` timestamp cutoffs. Plus #976 redaction at the write chokepoint: `message`, `stack`, and `details` are scrubbed of secret values before persist (re-running the sanitizer is idempotent, so facade-sanitized details are unharmed), and the DB-down console fallback redacts both the message and the caught driver error — the case where the error itself carries `DATABASE_URL` credentials. |
| `db.log-retention-policy.server.test.ts` | The singleton log-retention policy: `getLogRetentionPolicy` returns the existing `default` row, creates it when missing, and recovers the raced row after a P2002 create collision; `updateLogRetentionPolicy` persists normalized integer day counts; `runConfiguredLogRetention` deletes both audit and system rows past their windows and reports the counts; `runConfiguredLogRetentionIfDue` routes a failed sweep through `redactErrorForConsole` (PR #1291 review) — the sweep only ever fails on a DB error, which is exactly the error whose message embeds the connection string. |
| `db.cron-jobs.server.test.ts` | The cron job data layer: `listCronJobStatuses` merges known jobs with their latest run and active schedule overrides (Date→ISO conversion, `scheduleOverridden` flag), `startCronRun` returns `{ runId, created }` so only the INSERT winner spawns, `finishCronRun` UPDATE lifecycle, `getRecentCronJobRuns` timestamp serialization and null `finishedAt` handling, `updateCronSchedule`/`resetCronSchedule` override upsert/delete, and `triggerCronJobAsync` spawn args, exit-0 SUCCESS path, non-zero ERROR path, spawn error handling, and stdout capture. `finishCronRun` also redacts the persisted message (#976): cron scripts are spawned with the full `process.env`, so the stdout/stderr tail written to `cron_job_runs.message` can carry `DATABASE_URL` credentials or a token-bearing URL; non-secret diagnostic text is asserted to survive. `triggerCronJobAsync` pins the ordering (PR #1291 review): output is redacted *before* it is sliced to its trailing 1000 chars, since the redactor identifies a secret only by the credential-named key preceding it and a long value whose key fell outside the window used to survive as an unattributed tail. |

| `adhd-metrics.test.ts` | Tests `computeAdhdResponseMetrics` literal `**Top summary**` / `**Next?**` anchor detection, failure when `Next?` lacks bold markers, custom word-cap enforcement, `withStructuralPass` / `isStructuralCompliancePass` aggregation, and the 20-word clarification user-turn heuristic for `resolveAdhdResponseWordCap`. |
| `adhd-turn-profile.test.ts` | Tests `resolveAdhdTurnProfile` context-aware classification — greeting, confirmation after a tutor turn, S2.t2 multi-topic redirect, meta questions, and substantive questions/step follow-ups as full tutoring — including precedence regressions (substantive question/continuation and redirect outrank confirmation/greeting/meta, so acknowledgement-prefixed turns like "Great, what is gradient descent?" stay `full_tutoring` while bare acknowledgements stay `confirmation`), plus `getProfileRequirements` Dean/structure/word-cap expectations per profile. Also tests `userRequestedStepRecall`: detects the AI-2 facilitator-sheet turn 3 ("Go back to step 2 of the dish-washing procedure...") and other step-number phrasings with prior assistant context, and is false with no prior turn or no step-number reference (#1245). |
| `adhd-oversight.test.ts` | Tests `isAdhdOversightEnabled` env parsing, redirect preservation on baseline S2 turn 2, forward-offer vs comprehension-check extraction (including `Next?`-prefixed comprehension checks), last inline `Next?` matching, adversarial near-miss inputs that must not be promoted, `buildOverseenAssistantMessagesToPersist` tool-step preservation, deterministic fixes for archived S1/S2/S3 transcripts, LLM fallback + failure handling, ineligible-draft preservation (non-prose drafts pass through unchanged), over-cap LLM rejection, `isOversightEligibleDraft` guards, Track B fail-closed paths (`llm_retry` / `forced_deterministic`), the contentOk gate that rejects score-improving rewrites missing `**Next?**`, Markdown-preserving `truncateToWordCap` (keeps eduai-diagram fences; replaces oversized Sources footers so the forced wrap cannot overrun the word cap), forced-wrap diagram revalidation after truncation, and `forced_deterministic` contentOk/underCap gating (#1174). Also tests the `requireStepLadder` gate (#1245): a bare Top summary / Next? shell that used to pass through untouched now triggers an LLM rewrite on a step-recall turn and is accepted once it contains a real `### Step ladder`; the same shell still ships untouched when there is no step-recall request; and a minimal Step ladder skeleton is force-wrapped when LLM retries still miss it; and a reply that copies only the recalled step's line out of a multi-step prior ladder (renumbered down to "1.") is still caught and rejected, since the section-level comparison alone would miss it (#1245 review). |

| [`ssrf-guard.server.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/ssrf-guard.server.test.ts) | `assertPublicHostname` shared SSRF guard (#977): allows a hostname resolving to a public address; rejects loopback, RFC1918 private ranges, the cloud-metadata link-local address, IPv6 loopback/link-local/unique-local ranges, and an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address that unwraps to a private range; rejects when any one of multiple resolved records is blocked; and wraps a DNS resolution failure as `UnsafeHostError`. Extended in #1166: CGNAT `100.64/10`, `0.0.0.0`, IPv6 `::`, the empty-record-set branch, malformed-address fail-closed, and the reserved ranges (multicast `224/4`, `240/4`, broadcast, TEST-NET-1/2/3, `198.18/15`, `2001:db8::/32`, NAT64 `64:ff9b::/96`, IPv6 multicast `ff00::/8` — `ff02::1`, `ff05::1:3`, `ff0e::1`, the counterpart of the IPv4 `224/4` rule); **uncompressed IPv6 forms** — `0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1`, zero-padded `::1`, `0:0:0:0:0:ffff:7f00:1`, `0:0:0:0:0:ffff:a00:1`, deprecated `::127.0.0.1`, zone-index `fe80::1%eth0`, uppercase — which the previous string-matching logic allowed; public IPv6 addresses adjacent to blocked prefixes (`64:ff9c::1`, `2001:db9::1`) still resolve; malformed IPv6 (`gggg::1`, wrong group counts, double `::`) fails closed; `resolvePublicHost` returns a validated address and strips IPv6 brackets; and `assertPublicIpLiteral` rejects private literals synchronously without touching DNS. |
| [`pinned-dispatcher.server.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/pinned-dispatcher.server.test.ts) | `createPinnedLookup` DNS-rebinding defence (#1166): returns an array when called with `all: true` (undici's contract) and the `(address, family)` triple otherwise — the wrong shape makes the pin silently not apply; errors instead of connecting when the host resolves to a private address; pins to the address validated on the first resolution so a second, rebound answer cannot be substituted; surfaces DNS failures as a lookup error; and `getPinnedDispatcher` reuses one connection pool rather than opening one per request. |
| [`pinned-dispatcher.e2e.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/pinned-dispatcher.e2e.test.ts) | Same pinned dispatcher against a **real socket** and a local HTTP server, with no DNS mocking (#1166) — a lookup-shape unit test alone still passes if the dispatcher is never actually wired into `fetch`. Confirms a hostname resolving to loopback is refused with the guard's reason as the failure cause, and documents the deliberate limit that IP literals skip DNS resolution entirely (`net` only resolves names), so pinning is strictly the anti-rebinding layer while literals are covered by the save-time and pre-flight checks. |

Also tests that a redirect draft with the required cue/offer phrasing but that also explains the off-topic ask now triggers an LLM rewrite (instead of shipping unchanged), and the rewritten text no longer contains the bled-in facts (#1313) — including a draft that stays at exactly `MAX_REDIRECT_SENTENCES` and contains no urgency term, so the rewrite is provably triggered by the content check alone rather than the sentence cap or the urgency guard (review on #1421). |

| [`canvas-client.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/canvas-client.test.ts) | `parseAndValidateCanvasUrl` SSRF guard (HTTPS required except local dev hosts), `verifyCanvasCredentials` success/invalid token/Canvas errors/unreachable Canvas, test-mode roster mocks, `CanvasApiError`, `computeCanvasFilePublishState` (hidden/locked/lock_at/unlock_at boundary logic for determining if a Canvas file is currently published/visible to students), and the private-network host guard (#977) — `verifyCanvasCredentials`/`getCanvasCourseWithTerm` reject and never call `fetch` when the resolved Canvas host is disallowed, the guard is re-checked on every call (not cached from a prior success), and local-dev hosts (`localhost`) skip the guard entirely. Extended in #1166: `parseAndValidateCanvasUrl` rejects private/loopback/metadata IP literals and the `localhost` name at save time, rejects `https://` to a loopback literal (the dev allowance is `http`-only), and rejects the `http` dev-host allowance entirely when `NODE_ENV=production`; `verifyCanvasCredentials` and the paginated list path send `redirect: "manual"` and surface a 3xx as an error instead of following it to an unvalidated host; and Canvas requests carry a pinned `dispatcher` for real hosts but not for the local-dev Canvas, whose address the guard would reject. |
| [`canvas-client.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/canvas-client.test.ts) | `parseAndValidateCanvasUrl` SSRF guard (HTTPS required except local dev hosts), `verifyCanvasCredentials` success/invalid token/Canvas errors/unreachable Canvas, test-mode roster mocks, `CanvasApiError`, `computeCanvasFilePublishState` (hidden/locked/lock_at/unlock_at boundary logic for determining if a Canvas file is currently published/visible to students), and the private-network host guard (#977) — `verifyCanvasCredentials`/`getCanvasCourseWithTerm` reject and never call `fetch` when the resolved Canvas host is disallowed, the guard is re-checked on every call (not cached from a prior success), and local-dev hosts (`localhost`) skip the guard entirely. Extended in #1166: `parseAndValidateCanvasUrl` rejects private/loopback/metadata IP literals and the `localhost` name at save time, rejects `https://` to a loopback literal (the dev allowance is `http`-only), and rejects the `http` dev-host allowance entirely when `NODE_ENV=production`; `verifyCanvasCredentials` and the paginated list path send `redirect: "manual"` and surface a 3xx as an error instead of following it to an unvalidated host; and Canvas requests carry a pinned `dispatcher` for real hosts but not for the local-dev Canvas, whose address the guard would reject. Also covers the review follow-up on PR #1290: the local-dev free pass applies only to plain-`http` request URLs, so an `https` pagination Link header aimed at loopback is guarded (and rejected) even when the configured `canvasUrl` is the local dev Canvas, while an `http` Link header on that same Canvas still paginates without the guard, and the `https` request carries the pinned dispatcher the local-dev path omits. |

Edge-case audit #225: RAG-10 whitespace-only user message still prefetches but skips inject; RAG-11 exactly 20 vs 21 merged history messages (`slice(-20)` keeps newest 20); RAG-01/02 — when `findRelevantContent` throws for a course-intent query, the route no longer silently falls through to an ungrounded answer: it fails closed with `503` and a `classifyRagRetrievalError` code (`RAG_DIMENSION_MISMATCH` for dimension-mismatch messages, `RAG_RETRIEVAL_FAILED` otherwise) without calling `streamText`, on both the hybrid-inject and tool paths; a retrieval throw on a turn that never needed course grounding still degrades gracefully (no inject, normal streamed response). | [`db.cron-jobs.server.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/db.cron-jobs.server.test.ts) | The cron job data layer: `listCronJobStatuses` merges known jobs with their latest run and active schedule overrides (Date→ISO conversion, `scheduleOverridden` flag), `startCronRun` returns `{ runId, created }` so only the INSERT winner spawns, `finishCronRun` UPDATE lifecycle, `getRecentCronJobRuns` timestamp serialization and null `finishedAt` handling, `updateCronSchedule`/`resetCronSchedule` override upsert/delete, and `triggerCronJobAsync` spawn args, exit-0 SUCCESS path, non-zero ERROR path, spawn error handling, and stdout capture. `finishCronRun` also redacts the persisted message (#976): cron scripts are spawned with the full `process.env`, so the stdout/stderr tail written to `cron_job_runs.message` can carry `DATABASE_URL` credentials or a token-bearing URL; non-secret diagnostic text is asserted to survive. `triggerCronJobAsync` pins the ordering (PR #1291 review): output is redacted *before* it is sliced to its trailing 1000 chars, since the redactor identifies a secret only by the credential-named key preceding it and a long value whose key fell outside the window used to survive as an unattributed tail. |
| [`db.log-retention-policy.server.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/db.log-retention-policy.server.test.ts) | The singleton log-retention policy: `getLogRetentionPolicy` returns the existing `default` row, creates it when missing, and recovers the raced row after a P2002 create collision; `updateLogRetentionPolicy` persists normalized integer day counts; `runConfiguredLogRetention` deletes both audit and system rows past their windows and reports the counts; `runConfiguredLogRetentionIfDue` routes a failed sweep through `redactErrorForConsole` (PR #1291 review) — the sweep only ever fails on a DB error, which is exactly the error whose message embeds the connection string. |

Plus #976 redaction at the write chokepoint: `message`, `stack`, and `details` are scrubbed of secret values before persist (re-running the sanitizer is idempotent, so facade-sanitized details are unharmed), and the DB-down console fallback redacts both the message and the caught driver error — the case where the error itself carries `DATABASE_URL` credentials. |

Plus #225 RAG-05 `validateFileSignature`: accepts a real PDF/ZIP(DOCX/PPTX) declared under its matching MIME type and plain ASCII text declared as `text/plain`/`text/markdown`; rejects a mislabeled PDF/ZIP declared as the other's MIME, a PDF or ZIP/Office file mislabeled as `text/plain`, and binary (NUL-byte) noise mislabeled as `text/plain`; passes through unrecognized declared types (already covered by `validateFile`) and files with no `arrayBuffer` method unchanged. |

Flags two pre-existing source bugs found but not fixed (out of scope): `extractDocxText` calls mammoth with `{ arrayBuffer }`, which mammoth's Node build rejects — DOCX processing is currently broken; and `convertHtmlToMarkdown`'s `<ol>` handler uses a function replacer that returns a literal `"$1"` instead of the real list-item text. |

| [`logging.server.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/logging.server.test.ts) | The logging facade redaction: `logAuditAction` replaces credential- and PII-shaped keys (`password`, `phone`, `apiKey`, `secret`, `clientSecret`, `privateKey`, `jwt`, `databaseUrl`/`dbUrl`/`databaseUri`, `passcode`/`passphrase`, `otp`/`totp`/`dsn`/`mfa`/`mfaCode`/`mfaRecoveryCode`/`pin`, `sessionId`, etc.) with `[REDACTED]` while keeping accountability IDs and full emails; short tokens (`auth`/`pin`/`otp`/`dsn`/`totp`/`mfa`) use segment-exact matching so ordinary fields (`authorId`, `mapping`, `shippingAddress`, `hotPath`, `footprint`, `fieldsName`, `needsNormalization`) stay visible; MFA status flags (`mfaEnabled`/`mfaRequired`/`mfaEnrolled`/`mfaStatus`) stay visible via a safe-key allowlist; table-driven positive/negative cases; handles circular references (`[CIRCULAR]`) and Map/Set values without overflowing; `logSecurityEvent` forces the SECURITY category and still logs full emails; `logSystemError` routes through the centralized helper with redacted details. Also covers #976: all three facade calls now use value-level `sanitizeSensitiveData`, so a secret under an innocuous key is scrubbed too, and every `console.error` fallback passes the caught error through `redactErrorForConsole`. |
| [`logging.server.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/logging.server.test.ts) | The logging facade redaction: `logAuditAction` replaces credential- and PII-shaped keys (`password`, `phone`, `apiKey`, `secret`, `clientSecret`, `privateKey`, etc.) with `[REDACTED]` while keeping accountability IDs and full emails, handles circular references (`[CIRCULAR]`) and Map/Set values without overflowing; `logSecurityEvent` forces the SECURITY category and still logs full emails; `logSystemError` routes through the centralized helper with redacted details. Also covers #976: all three facade calls now use value-level `sanitizeSensitiveData`, so a secret under an innocuous key is scrubbed too, and every `console.error` fallback passes the caught error through `redactErrorForConsole`. |
| [`redact.server.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/redact.server.test.ts) | Shared redactor (#979/#976 patterns): value-level scrubbing of Bearer/Basic credentials (anchored to `Authorization:` context or 16+-char tokens so prose like "Basic setup complete" survives — PR #1116 review), Cookie/Set-Cookie and X-Api-Key header lines, token query params, and URL userinfo; HAR-style `{ name, value }` header redaction; combined key+value `sanitizeSensitiveData` (the weaker key-only `sanitizeDetails` was removed in #976 — value scrubbing is a strict superset); `redactErrorForConsole` flattening an `Error` to a redacted plain shape; JSON diagnostic-log redaction; linear-time guard so long non-URL blobs do not ReDoS. Structured `key=value` / `"key": "value"` pairs get their own block (PR #1291 review): env/shell assignment dumps, a nested `{…}` / `[…]` literal under a credential key redacted whole (bracket counting ignores brackets inside quoted values, spans newlines, and redacts to the end when the literal is truncated), quoted values containing escaped quotes, idempotence across every shape, and linearity on both a dense separator blob and repeated unterminated literals. A fail-closed block covers the second review round on #1291: a URL value is redacted through its own query string (`apiKey=https://host/path?foo=secret` no longer leaves `?foo=secret` behind) while a following `&param` survives, an unterminated quoted value or a value opening with a delimiter is redacted to the end of the line rather than passed through, a genuinely empty value (`apiKey=,`) is left alone, and repeated unterminated quoted values stay linear. Also pins the `client_secret` query param as redacted and `?code=` as deliberately *not* redacted, since course codes are logged as `?code=CPSC110`. |

| [`users-api-pagination.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/unit/users-api-pagination.test.ts) | #1144 regression coverage: a course manager may use the paginated users API only to search active STUDENT candidates for a managed course, excluding already-enrolled users; instructors cannot use the general directory and candidate queries must carry the active-student constraint. `courseId` requests branch onto a narrow `[count, findMany]` query before any Prisma call is built, asserting the `findMany` `select` is exactly `{ id, name, email }` and the raw response never carries `stats`, `taCourseIds`, `authorizedUnits`, `_count`, `role`, or `isActive` — the admin-shaped query (platform counts, TA/role/unit fields) is never run for these requests, not merely filtered out after the fact. |

## EduAI Integration Tests

**Path:** `apps/core/app/tests/integration/`

| Test file | What it tests |
|-----------|---------------|
| `invitations.integration.test.ts` | The admin invitation workflow on the test DB with the real Better Auth handler: create (admin-only, stores only the token hash, emails the accept link, supersedes prior PENDING invites for the email, 409 when the user exists), list hiding `tokenHash`, revoke and resend (token rotation kills the old link; 404/409 guards), and the accept flow driven through the user-facing page route (`routes/auth/accept-invitation`) — a real logged-in account with the invited role and `authorizedUnits` via a 302 redirect to `/dashboard`, friendly form errors (not status codes) for invalid/expired/revoked tokens (incl. the `INVITATION_REVOKED` code) and squatted emails, and sign-up rollback when the promote step fails so the same invite link still works on retry; plus the unit-admin flow (#686) — a UNIT_ADMIN is 403'd while `unitAdmins.canInvite` is off, invites a STUDENT who then accepts into a STUDENT account when it's on, is `FORBIDDEN_ROLE` when inviting an ADMIN, sees only the invites they sent, and 404s when revoking another inviter's invite; plus the §567 UBC gate — admin and unit-admin invites with a non-UBC email are 400'd, and a direct public `sign-up/email` through the real Better Auth handler rejects a non-UBC address (400, no account) while creating an account for a UBC one (proving the before-hook reads the email, not a blanket block). |
| `invitations.integration.test.ts` | The admin invitation workflow on the test DB with the real Better Auth handler: create (admin-only, stores only the token hash, emails the accept link, supersedes prior PENDING invites for the email, 409 when the user exists, an ADMIN inviting a STUDENT), list hiding `tokenHash`, revoke and resend (token rotation kills the old link; 404/409 guards), and the accept flow driven through the user-facing page route (`routes/auth/accept-invitation`) — a real logged-in account with the invited role and `authorizedUnits` via a 302 redirect to `/dashboard` (including a STUDENT invite that yields a user with no authorized units), friendly form errors (not status codes) for invalid/expired/revoked tokens (incl. the `INVITATION_REVOKED` code) and squatted emails, and sign-up rollback when the promote step fails so the same invite link still works on retry; plus the unit-admin flow (#686) — a UNIT_ADMIN is 403'd while `unitAdmins.canInvite` is off, invites a STUDENT who then accepts into a STUDENT account when it's on, is `FORBIDDEN_ROLE` when inviting an ADMIN, sees only the invites they sent, and 404s when revoking another inviter's invite. |
| `courses.rag-settings.integration.test.ts` | `GET`/`PATCH /api/courses/:id/rag-settings on the test DB: auth (401/403), the #975 course-access IDOR fix (403 for an enrolled student on GET, 403 for an instructor with no relationship to the course on both GET and PATCH, DB left untouched), validation (422 out-of-range), persist and read back `ragTopK`/`ragSimilarityThreshold`, null clears overrides, 404 for unknown courses. |

Adds the #1166 SSRF regression at the connect endpoint: an instructor cannot save a Canvas URL pointing at an internal host (`10.0.0.5`, `192.168.1.1`, `169.254.169.254`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`, `localhost`) — 400, credentials never verified, nothing persisted — including with `isTestMode: true`, the path that previously skipped the only host check. |

| [`ivfflat-filtered-recall.integration.test.ts`](apps/core/app/tests/integration/ivfflat-filtered-recall.integration.test.ts) | Filtered ANN recall regression for the `material_embeddings` ivfflat index (#940): seeds a real Postgres + pgvector table skewed 4000:4 (a large unrelated "noise" course vs. a small target course clustered near the query vector), then proves `findRelevantContent()` — the real function, not a SQL-shape assertion — still returns all 4 target-course rows with `RAG_IVFFLAT_PROBES=1` (relies on `ivfflat.iterative_scan = relaxed_order` + a bounded `ivfflat.max_probes` to keep expanding the scan past the noise course's lists), an exact-scan sanity check for the same seed data, and a control case proving the fix doesn't just return everything (the noise course's opposite-direction embeddings still return zero rows). |
